# ISPE Baseline® Guide:

# Commissioning and Qualification

# Appendix 17

# Critical Aspects (CAs) (ASTM E2500-13 [5])

Functions, features, abilities, and performance or characteristics necessary for the manufacturing process and systems to ensure consistent product quality and patient safety.

# Critical Design Elements (CDEs)

Design functions or features of an engineered system that are necessary to consistently manufacture products with the desired quality attributes. Examples of automation design functions include alarms and data management. Examples of engineering design features include components, instruments, and materials of construction. CDEs are identified and documented based on technical understanding of the product CQAs, process CPPs, and equipment design/automation. CDEs are verified through C&Q.

# Critical Process Parameter (CPP) (ICH Q8 [4])

A process parameter whose variability has an impact on a critical quality attribute and therefore should be monitored or controlled to ensure the process produces the desired quality.

# Critical Quality Attribute (CQA) (ICH Q8 [4])

A physical, chemical, biological or microbiological property or characteristic that should be within an appropriate limit, range, or distribution to ensure the desired product quality. CQAs are generally associated with the drug substance, excipients, intermediates (in-process materials) and drug product.

# Design Development

Engineering process by which the design generation is managed, from the conceptual level through increasing detail to final design documentation. The process is based on documented user requirements.

# Design Qualification (DQ)

A documented process that verifies the proposed design of the facilities, systems, utilities, and equipment is suitable for the intended purpose.

# Design Review (DR) (ISO/IEC/IEEE 24765 [14])

Formal, documented, comprehensive, and systematic examination of a design to determine if the design meets the applicable requirements, to identify problems, and to propose solutions.

This Document is licensed to Mr. Rodolfo Russo

# Detailed Design

Design stage that includes all further design activities required to reach the construction or purchasing stage. The documents required for construction bidding and contracting as well as system and equipment purchase, fabrication, installation, and testing are produced. Examples include final locations of equipment, engineering calculations for sizing, specifications, quality plan, and safety plan. Detailed design needs to result in documented DR/DQ.

# Deviation

In the context of this Guide, a departure from specification, or a critical discrepancy that cannot be corrected, that affects a CA/CDE. Deviations require Quality Unit approval prior to closure.

For individual use only. © Copyright ISPE 2019. All rights reserved.
---
# ISPE Baseline® Guide: Appendix 17

# Commissioning and Qualification

# Direct Impact System

A system that directly impacts product CQAs, or directly impacts the quality of the product delivered by a critical utility system. All other systems are considered to be not direct impact.

# Discrepancy (FDA 2006 Quality Systems Guidance [15])

Datum or result outside of the expected range; an unfulfilled requirement; may be called non-conformity, defect, deviation, out-of-specification, out-of-limit, out-of-trend.

# Engineering Change Management (ECM)

A process by which a qualified representative(s) reviews proposed or actual changes for their impact, approves or denies the requests, and manages and tracks their implementation. ECM is good engineering practice to effectively manage the project scope and execution.

# Engineering Quality Process

For the purposes of this Guide, the term used to describe the process within the PQS that covers C&Q.

# Expected Results

The result that the test case should produce based on the requirements.

# Factory Acceptance Test (FAT)

Inspection and static and/or dynamic testing of manufacturing systems (or major system components) performed to approve the systems for delivery and to support qualification of the systems. FATs are conducted and documented at the vendor site and are approved by the vendor team in collaboration with the owner designee(s).

# Good Engineering Practice (GEP)

Established engineering methods and standards that are applied throughout a project lifecycle to deliver appropriate, cost-effective solutions.

# Inspection

In the context of this Guide, the process of visually verifying compliance with the design specifications.

Note: This Document is licensed to Inspections for code compliance are outside the scope of this Guide.

# Installation Qualification (IQ)

A documented process that verifies that the facilities, systems, utilities, and equipment, as installed or modified, comply with the approved design and the manufacturer’s recommendations.

# Installation Verification (IV)

The activity of reviewing, inspecting, testing, checking, auditing, or otherwise establishing and documenting whether items, processes, services, or documents conform to specified requirements.

# Legacy Systems

For the purposes of this Guide, the term used to describe systems previously qualified using methodologies other than those presented in this Guide.

For individual use only. © Copyright ISPE 2019. All rights reserved.
---
# ISPE Baseline® Guide:

# Commissioning and Qualification

# Appendix 17

# Non-Conformity (FDA 2006 Quality Systems Guidance [15])

A deficiency in a characteristic, product specification, process parameter, record, or procedure that renders the quality of a product unacceptable, indeterminate, or not according to specified requirements.

# Off-the-Shelf (also known as catalog)

Equipment that is available in a standard configuration without customization.

# Operating Limits

The minimum and/or maximum defined operating values for a system that will ensure product and safety requirements are met.

# Operational Qualification (OQ)

A documented process that verifies that the facilities, systems, utilities, and equipment, as installed or modified, perform as intended throughout the anticipated operating ranges.

# Operational Verification (OV)

The activity of reviewing, testing, checking, or otherwise establishing and documenting that a system operates according to written and pre-approved specifications throughout all specified operating ranges.

# Performance Qualification (PQ)

A documented process that verifies that the facilities, systems, utilities, and equipment, as connected together, can perform effectively and reproducibly, based on the approved process method and product specification.

# Performance Testing

The process by which the performance of interdependent systems is demonstrated as within the required tolerances, the output of interdependent systems is demonstrated as delivering the required duty or capacity, and the interdependent functions of systems are demonstrated to be as specified and appropriate.

# Pharmaceutical Quality System (PQS) (ICH Q10 [9] based upon ISO 9000 [36])

Management system to direct and control a pharmaceutical company with regard to quality.

# Pre-Delivery Inspection (PDI)

Inspection and testing of systems (or major system components) before delivery to site.

# Process Performance Qualification (PPQ) (FDA 2011 Process Validation Guidance [2])

The PPQ combines the actual facility, utilities, equipment (each now qualified), and the trained personnel with the commercial manufacturing process, control procedures, and components to produce commercial batches. A successful PPQ will confirm the process design and demonstrate that the commercial manufacturing process performs as expected.

# Process Validation (PV) (FDA 2011 Process Validation Guidance [2])

The collection and evaluation of data, from the process design stage through commercial production, which establishes scientific evidence that a process is capable of consistently delivering quality products.

This Document is licensed to Mr. Rodolfo Russo RHO, MILANO

# ISPE Baseline® Guide: Appendix 17

# Commissioning and Qualification

# Qualification

For the purpose of this Guide, a process to demonstrate and document that the critical manufacturing facilities, systems, utilities, and equipment are suitable for the intended purpose.

# Quality (FDA 2006 Quality Systems Guidance [15])

The degree to which a set of inherent properties of a product, system or process fulfills requirements.

# Quality Assurance (QA) (FDA 2006 Quality Systems Guidance [15])

Proactive and retrospective activities that provide confidence that requirements are fulfilled.

# Quality Risk Management (QRM) (ICH Q9 [3])

A systematic process for the assessment, control, communication and review of risks to the quality of the drug (medicinal) product across the product lifecycle.

# Quality System

Planned and systematic activities necessary to provide adequate confidence that a product, process, or service satisfies specified quality requirements.

# Quality Unit (FDA 2011 Process Validation Guidance [2])

A group organized within an organization to promote quality in general practice.

# Sequence of Operation(s)

A detailed description of the system start-up, normal operation and cleaning (as applicable), process monitoring, data acquisition and archive, alarm conditions and response, and shutdown.

# Site Acceptance Test (SAT)

Inspection and static and/or dynamic testing of manufacturing systems (or major system components) performed to support the qualification of a system. SATs are conducted, documented, and approved at the manufacturing site by the owner team in collaboration with the supplier designee(s).

# Specifications (ICH Q7 [37])

A list of tests, references to analytical procedures, and appropriate acceptance criteria that are numerical limits, ranges, or other criteria for the test described. It establishes the set of criteria to which a material should conform to be considered acceptable for its intended use. “Conformance to specification” means that the material, when tested according to the listed analytical procedures, will meet the listed acceptance criteria.

# Stakeholders (ICH Q9 [3])

Any individual, group or organization that can affect, be affected by, or perceive itself to be affected by a risk, action, or decision.

# Standard Operating Procedure (SOP)

Written and approved procedures to ensure that activities are performed the same way each time.

This Document is licensed to Mr. Rodolfo Russo

ID number: 364201

Downloaded on: 7/18/20 2:03 AM

For individual use only. © Copyright ISPE 2019. All rights reserved.

# ISPE Baseline® Guide:

# Commissioning and Qualification

# Appendix 17

# Subject Matter Expert (SME) (ASTM E2500-13 [5])

Individuals with specific expertise in a particular area or field (for example, quality unit, engineering, automation, development, operations, and so forth).

# Suitable for the Intended Purpose

The system requirements are defined, the design is appropriate for control of the manufacturing process, and the system is fabricated and installed as designed and performs as intended throughout the anticipated operating ranges.

# System Boundary

A limit drawn around a system to logically define what is and is not included in the system.

# System Owner

The person ultimately responsible for the availability, support, and maintenance of a system and for the security of the data residing on that system.

# Systems

For the purposes of this Guide, the term used to refer to facilities, systems, utilities, and/or equipment.

# Testing

Process by which adjustments to, and regulation of, individual systems are demonstrated as within the required tolerances, system components are demonstrated as delivering the required capacity or duty, the functions of the system are demonstrated as delivering the required capacity of duty, the functions of the system are demonstrated to be as specified and appropriate.

# User Requirements Specification (URS)

A description of the requirements of the system in terms of product to be manufactured, required throughput and conditions in which the product should be made.

# Validation (FDA 1987 Process Validation Guidance [38]⁶)

Establishing documented evidence which provides a high degree of assurance that a specific process will consistently produce a product meeting its predetermined specifications and quality attributes.

# Vendor (also known as a Supplier)

For the purposes of this Guide, the term used to describe the organization providing the system or providing the contract services to execute the C&QRHO program for the organization that owns the system.

# Verification

An activity that is performed within the C&Q process to document that the manufacturing facilities, systems, utilities, and equipment are suitable for the intended purpose.

6 The FDA 1987 Process Validation Guidance [38] has been replaced by the FDA 2011 Process Validation Guidance [2].
